The following description:

- When a trap occurs in HS-mode or U-mode, it goes to M-mode, unless delegated by medeleg or mideleg.
- When a trap occurs in VS-mode or VU-mode, it goes to M-mode, unless delegated by medeleg or mideleg.
- When a trap is taken into M-mode, fields MPV and MPP in mstatus (or mstatush) are set according to Table 52.
- A trap into M-mode also writes fields GVA, MPIE, and MIE in mstatus / mstatush.
- Upon trap return, MPV is ignored when MPP=3.

Table 52. Value of mstatus / mstatush fields MPV and MPP after a trap into M-mode.

| Previous Mode | MPV | MPP |
|---------------|-----|-----|
| U-mode        | 0   | 0   |
| HS-mode       | 0   | 1   |
| M-mode        | 0   | 3   |
| VU-mode       | 1   | 0   |
| VS-mode       | 1   | 1   |
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: When a trap is taken into M-mode from U-mode, the mstatus/mstatush field MPV is set to 0 and the mstatus/mstatush field MPP is set to 0.
RULE 2: When a trap is taken into M-mode from HS-mode, the mstatus/mstatush field MPV is set to 0 and the mstatus/mstatush field MPP is set to 1.
RULE 3: When a trap is taken into M-mode from M-mode, the mstatus/mstatush field MPV is set to 0 and the mstatus/mstatush field MPP is set to 3.
RULE 4: When a trap is taken into M-mode from VU-mode, the mstatus/mstatush field MPV is set to 1 and the mstatus/mstatush field MPP is set to 0.
RULE 5: When a trap is taken into M-mode from VS-mode, the mstatus/mstatush field MPV is set to 1 and the mstatus/mstatush field MPP is set to 1.
RULE 6: A trap into M-mode writes the mstatus/mstatush field GVA.
RULE 7: A trap into M-mode writes the mstatus/mstatush field MPIE.
RULE 8: A trap into M-mode writes the mstatus/mstatush field MIE.
RULE 9: Upon trap return with the mstatus/mstatush field MPP equal to 3, the mstatus/mstatush field MPV is ignored.

The following description:

- To store the previous ELP state on trap delivery to M-mode, an MPELP bit is provided in the mstatus CSR.
- To store the previous ELP state on trap delivery to S/HS-mode, an SPELP bit is provided in the mstatus CSR.
- The SPELP bit in mstatus can be accessed through the sstatus CSR.
- When a trap is taken into privilege mode x, the x PELP is set to ELP and ELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.
- An MRET or SRET instruction is used to return from a trap in M-mode or S-mode, respectively. When executing an x RET instruction, if the new privilege mode is y, then ELP is set to the value of x PELP if y LPE (see Section 23.1.1) is 1; otherwise, it is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED; x PELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.
- The trap handler in privilege mode x must save the x PELP bit and the x7 register before performing an indirect call/jump if xLPE=1.
- If the privilege mode x can respond to interrupts and xLPE=1, then the trap handler should also save these values before enabling interrupts.
- The trap handler in privilege mode x must restore the saved x PELP bit and the x7 register before executing the x RET instruction to return from a trap.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The mstatus CSR provides an MPELP bit to store the previous ELP state on trap delivery to M-mode.
RULE 2: The mstatus CSR provides an SPELP bit to store the previous ELP state on trap delivery to S/HS-mode.
RULE 3: The SPELP bit in the mstatus CSR can be accessed through the sstatus CSR.
RULE 4: When a trap is taken into M-mode, the MPELP bit in the mstatus CSR is set to ELP and ELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.
RULE 5: When a trap is taken into S/HS-mode, the SPELP bit in the mstatus CSR is set to ELP and ELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.
RULE 6: When an MRET instruction executes and the new privilege mode y has yLPE equal to 1, ELP is set to the value of the MPELP bit in the mstatus CSR and the MPELP bit in the mstatus CSR is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.
RULE 7: When an SRET instruction executes and the new privilege mode y has yLPE equal to 1, ELP is set to the value of the SPELP bit in the mstatus CSR and the SPELP bit in the mstatus CSR is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.
RULE 8: When an MRET instruction executes and the new privilege mode y does not have yLPE equal to 1, ELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED and the MPELP bit in the mstatus CSR is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.
RULE 9: When an SRET instruction executes and the new privilege mode y does not have yLPE equal to 1, ELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED and the SPELP bit in the mstatus CSR is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.
RULE 10: If xLPE equals 1, the trap handler in privilege mode x must save the xPELP bit in the mstatus CSR and the x7 register before performing an indirect call or jump.
RULE 11: If privilege mode x can respond to interrupts and xLPE equals 1, the trap handler should also save the xPELP bit in the mstatus CSR and the x7 register before enabling interrupts.
RULE 12: The trap handler in privilege mode x must restore the saved xPELP bit in the mstatus CSR and the x7 register before executing the xRET instruction to return from a trap.

The following description:

- In the mstatus register, the TVM (Trap Virtual Memory) bit is a WARL field that supports intercepting supervisor virtual-memory management operations.

RULE 4: When mstatus.TVM=0 and execution is in S-mode, an attempt to read the satp CSR is permitted.
RULE 5: When mstatus.TVM=0 and execution is in S-mode, an attempt to write the satp CSR is permitted.
RULE 6: Trapping satp accesses provides the hooks necessary to lazily populate shadow page tables.

The following description:

- The satp CSR is an SXLEN-bit read/write register, formatted as shown in Figure 63 for SXLEN=32 and Figure 64 for SXLEN=64, which controls supervisor-mode address translation and protection.
- This register holds the physical page number (PPN) of the root page table, i.e., its supervisor physical address divided by 4 KiB; an address space identifier (ASID), which facilitates address-translation fences on a per-addressspace basis; and the MODE field, which selects the current address-translation scheme.
- Further details on the access to this register are described in Section 3.1.6.6.

Figure 63. Supervisor address translation and protection ( satp ) register when SXLEN=32.
| 31 | 30 | 22 21 |
| MODE (WARL) | ASID (WARL) | PPN (WARL) |
| 1 | 9 | 22 |

Figure 64. Supervisor address translation and protection ( satp ) register when SXLEN=64, for MODE values Bare, Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57.
| 63 | 60 59 | 44 43 |
| MODE (WARL) | ASID (WARL) | PPN (WARL) |
| 4 | 16 | 44 |

- Storing a PPN in satp, rather than a physical address, supports a physical address space larger than 4 GiB for RV32.
- The satp.PPN field might not be capable of holding all physical page numbers. Some platform standards might place constraints on the values satp.PPN may assume, e.g., by requiring that all physical page numbers corresponding to main memory be representable.
- We store the ASID and the page table base address in the same CSR to allow the pair to be changed atomically on a context switch.
- Swapping them non-atomically could pollute the old virtual address space with new translations, or vice-versa.
- This approach also slightly reduces the cost of a context switch.

Table 40 shows the encodings of the MODE field when SXLEN=32 and SXLEN=64.

Table 40. Encoding of satp MODE field.

SXLEN=32:
| Value | Name | Description |
| 0 | Bare | No translation or protection. |
| 1 | Sv32 | Page-based 32-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.3). |

SXLEN=64:
| Value | Name | Description |
| 0 | Bare | No translation or protection. |
| 1-7 | - | Reserved for standard use |
| 8 | Sv39 | Page-based 39-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.4). |
| 9 | Sv48 | Page-based 48-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.5). |
| 10 | Sv57 | Page-based 57-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.6). |
| 11 | Sv64 | Reserved for page-based 64-bit virtual addressing. |
| 12-13 | - | Reserved for standard use |
| 14-15 | - | Designated for custom use |

- When MODE=Bare, supervisor virtual addresses are equal to supervisor physical addresses, and there is no additional memory protection beyond the physical memory protection scheme described in Section 3.7.
- To select MODE=Bare, software must write zero to the remaining fields of satp (bits 30-0 when SXLEN=32, or bits 59-0 when SXLEN=64).
- Attempting to select MODE=Bare with a nonzero pattern in the remaining fields has an UNSPECIFIED effect on the value that the remaining fields assume and an UNSPECIFIED effect on address translation and protection behavior.
- When SXLEN=32, the satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]=3 are designated for custom use, whereas the encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]!=3 are reserved for future standard use.
- When SXLEN=64, all satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare are reserved for future standard use.
- Version 1.11 of this standard stated that the remaining fields in satp had no effect when MODE=Bare. Making these fields reserved facilitates future definition of additional translation and protection modes, particularly in RV32, for which all patterns of the existing MODE field have already been allocated.
- When SXLEN=32, the only other valid setting for MODE is Sv32, a paged virtual-memory scheme described in Section 12.3.
- When SXLEN=64, three paged virtual-memory schemes are defined: Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57, described in Section 12.4, Section 12.5, and Section 12.6, respectively. One additional scheme, Sv64, will be defined in a later version of this specification. The remaining MODE settings are reserved for future use and may define different interpretations of the other fields in satp.
- Implementations are not required to support all MODE settings, and if satp is written with an unsupported MODE, the entire write has no effect; no fields in satp are modified.
- The number of ASID bits is UNSPECIFIED and may be zero.
- The number of implemented ASID bits, termed ASIDLEN, may be determined by writing one to every bit position in the ASID field, then reading back the value in satp to see which bit positions in the ASID field hold a one.
- The least-significant bits of ASID are implemented first: that is, if ASIDLEN > 0, ASID[ASIDLEN-1:0] is writable.
- The maximal value of ASIDLEN, termed ASIDMAX, is 9 for Sv32 or 16 for Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57.
- The satp CSR is considered active when the effective privilege mode is S-mode or U-mode.
- Executions of the address-translation algorithm may only begin using a given value of satp when satp is active.
- Translations that began while satp was active are not required to complete or terminate when satp is no longer active, unless an SFENCE.VMA instruction matching the address and ASID is executed.
- The SFENCE.VMA instruction must be used to ensure that updates to the address-translation data structures are observed by subsequent implicit reads to those structures by a hart.
- Note that writing satp does not imply any ordering constraints between page-table updates and subsequent address translations, nor does it imply any invalidation of address-translation caches.
- If the new address space's page tables have been modified, or if an ASID is reused, it may be necessary to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction (see Section 12.2.1) after, or in some cases before, writing satp.
- Not imposing upon implementations to flush address-translation caches upon satp writes reduces the cost of context switches, provided a sufficiently large ASID space.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The satp CSR is an SXLEN-bit read/write register.
RULE 2: The satp CSR controls supervisor-mode address translation and protection.
RULE 3: When SXLEN=32, satp has a 1-bit WARL MODE field in bit 31, a 9-bit WARL ASID field in bits 30:22, and a 22-bit WARL PPN field in bits 21:0.
RULE 4: When SXLEN=64 and satp.MODE is Bare, Sv39, Sv48, or Sv57, satp has a 4-bit WARL MODE field in bits 63:60, a 16-bit WARL ASID field in bits 59:44, and a 44-bit WARL PPN field in bits 43:0.
RULE 5: The satp.PPN field holds the physical page number of the root page table, which is its supervisor physical address divided by 4 KiB.
RULE 6: The satp.ASID field facilitates address-translation fences on a per-address-space basis.
RULE 7: The satp.MODE field selects the current address-translation scheme.
RULE 8: Storing a PPN in satp rather than a physical address supports a physical address space larger than 4 GiB for RV32.
RULE 9: The satp.PPN field might not be capable of holding all physical page numbers.
RULE 10: Some platform standards might place constraints on the values satp.PPN may assume, for example by requiring that all physical page numbers corresponding to main memory be representable.
RULE 11: The satp CSR stores the ASID and the page table base address in the same CSR to allow the pair to be changed atomically on a context switch.
RULE 12: Swapping the satp.ASID and the satp page table base address non-atomically could pollute the old virtual address space with new translations or vice versa.
RULE 13: Storing the ASID and page table base address together in satp slightly reduces the cost of a context switch.
RULE 14: When SXLEN=32, satp.MODE value 0 selects Bare, which provides no translation or protection.
RULE 15: When SXLEN=32, satp.MODE value 1 selects Sv32, which provides page-based 32-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 16: When SXLEN=32, the only valid satp.MODE settings are Bare and Sv32.
RULE 17: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE value 0 selects Bare, which provides no translation or protection.
RULE 18: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE values 1 through 7 are reserved for standard use.
RULE 19: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE value 8 selects Sv39, which provides page-based 39-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 20: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE value 9 selects Sv48, which provides page-based 48-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 21: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE value 10 selects Sv57, which provides page-based 57-bit virtual addressing.
RULE 22: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE value 11 is reserved for Sv64, a page-based 64-bit virtual addressing scheme that will be defined in a later version of the specification.
RULE 23: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE values 12 and 13 are reserved for standard use.
RULE 24: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE values 14 and 15 are designated for custom use.
RULE 25: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE settings that are reserved for future use may define different interpretations of the other fields in satp.
RULE 26: When satp.MODE is Bare, supervisor virtual addresses are equal to supervisor physical addresses.
RULE 27: When satp.MODE is Bare, there is no additional memory protection beyond the physical memory protection scheme.
RULE 28: To select satp.MODE=Bare when SXLEN=32, software must write zero to satp bits 30-0.
RULE 29: To select satp.MODE=Bare when SXLEN=64, software must write zero to satp bits 59-0.
RULE 30: Attempting to select satp.MODE=Bare with a nonzero pattern in satp bits 30-0 when SXLEN=32 has an UNSPECIFIED effect on the value that the remaining fields assume and an UNSPECIFIED effect on address translation and protection behavior.
RULE 31: Attempting to select satp.MODE=Bare with a nonzero pattern in satp bits 59-0 when SXLEN=64 has an UNSPECIFIED effect on the value that the remaining fields assume and an UNSPECIFIED effect on address translation and protection behavior.
RULE 32: When SXLEN=32, satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]=3 are designated for custom use.
RULE 33: When SXLEN=32, satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]!=3 are reserved for future standard use.
RULE 34: When SXLEN=64, all satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare are reserved for future standard use.
RULE 35: When SXLEN=64, three paged virtual-memory schemes are defined for satp.MODE: Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57.
RULE 36: When SXLEN=64, one additional paged virtual-memory scheme for satp.MODE, Sv64, will be defined in a later version of this specification.
RULE 37: Implementations are not required to support all satp.MODE settings.
RULE 38: If satp is written with an unsupported MODE, the entire write has no effect and no fields in satp are modified.
RULE 39: The number of implemented satp.ASID bits is UNSPECIFIED and may be zero.
RULE 40: The number of implemented satp.ASID bits, termed ASIDLEN, may be determined by writing one to every bit position in the satp.ASID field and then reading back satp to see which satp.ASID bit positions hold a one.
RULE 41: For satp.ASID, the least-significant bits are implemented first.
RULE 42: If the number of implemented satp.ASID bits, termed ASIDLEN, is greater than zero, satp.ASID[ASIDLEN-1:0] is writable.
RULE 43: For satp with Sv32 selected, the maximal value of ASIDLEN, termed ASIDMAX, is 9.
RULE 44: For satp with Sv39, Sv48, or Sv57 selected, the maximal value of ASIDLEN, termed ASIDMAX, is 16.
RULE 45: The satp CSR is considered active when the effective privilege mode is S-mode or U-mode.
RULE 46: Executions of the address-translation algorithm may only begin using a given value of satp when satp is active.
RULE 47: Translations that began while satp was active are not required to complete or terminate when satp is no longer active, unless an SFENCE.VMA instruction matching the address and ASID is executed.
RULE 48: Writing satp does not imply any ordering constraints between page-table updates and subsequent address translations.
RULE 49: Writing satp does not imply any invalidation of address-translation caches.
RULE 50: If the new address space's page tables have been modified or if an ASID is reused, it may be necessary to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction after, or in some cases before, writing satp.
RULE 51: Not requiring address-translation caches to be flushed upon satp writes reduces the cost of context switches, provided a sufficiently large ASID space.
RULE 52: When SXLEN=32, all patterns of the existing satp.MODE field have already been allocated.
RULE 53: Reserving the satp fields other than MODE for MODE=Bare facilitates future definition of additional translation and protection modes, particularly in RV32.

The following description:

- This property applies even if the virtual-address width for that translation differs from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.

- Implementations must only perform implicit reads of the translation data structures pointed to by the current contents of the satp register or a subsequent valid (V=1) translation data structure entry, and must only raise exceptions for implicit accesses that are generated as a result of instruction execution, not those that are performed speculatively.

- Changing satp.MODE from Bare to other modes and vice versa also takes effect immediately, without the need to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction. Likewise, changes to satp.ASID take effect immediately.

- When software recycles an ASID (i.e., reassociates it with a different page table), it should first change satp to point to the new page table using the recycled ASID, then execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1 = x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID. Alternatively, software can execute the same SFENCE.VMA instruction while a different ASID is loaded into satp, provided the next time satp is loaded with the recycled ASID, it is simultaneously loaded with the new page table.

- If the implementation does not provide ASIDs, or software chooses to always use ASID 0, then after every satp write, software should execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1 = x0. In the common case that no global translations have been modified, rs2 should be set to a register other than x0 but which contains the value zero, so that global translations are not flushed.

- For implementations that make satp.MODE read-only zero (always Bare), attempts to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction might raise an illegal-instruction exception.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: A property applies even if the virtual-address width for a translation differs from the virtual-address width currently specified by satp.MODE.
RULE 2: Implementations must only perform implicit reads of translation data structures pointed to by the current contents of the satp register or by a subsequent valid (V=1) translation data structure entry.
RULE 3: For implicit accesses to translation data structures pointed to by the current contents of satp, implementations must only raise exceptions for implicit accesses generated as a result of instruction execution, not those performed speculatively.
RULE 4: Changing satp.MODE from Bare to a mode other than Bare takes effect immediately without the need to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction.
RULE 5: Changing satp.MODE from a mode other than Bare to Bare takes effect immediately without the need to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction.
RULE 6: Changes to satp.ASID take effect immediately.
RULE 7: When software recycles an ASID by reassociating it with a different page table, software should first change satp to point to the new page table using the recycled ASID and then execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1 = x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID.
RULE 8: Alternatively, when software recycles an ASID, software can execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction with rs1 = x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID while a different ASID is loaded into satp, provided that the next time satp is loaded with the recycled ASID it is simultaneously loaded with the new page table.
RULE 9: If the implementation does not provide ASIDs or software chooses to always use ASID 0, then after every satp write software should execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1 = x0.
RULE 10: After every satp write when the implementation does not provide ASIDs or software chooses to always use ASID 0, in the common case that no global translations have been modified, rs2 should be set to a register other than x0 that contains the value zero so that global translations are not flushed.
RULE 11: For implementations that make satp.MODE read-only zero so that satp.MODE is always Bare, attempts to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction might raise an illegal-instruction exception.

The following description:

- When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of the satp register:
  - supervisor virtual addresses are translated into supervisor physical addresses via a two-level page table.
  - the 20-bit VPN is translated into a 22-bit physical page number (PPN), while the 12-bit page offset is untranslated.

- The physical page number of the root page table is stored in the satp register.

- For example, consider an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address:

The following description:

- In the virtual address translation process, a virtual address va is translated into a physical address pa as follows: Let a be satp.ppn x PAGESIZE, and let i = LEVELS-1.
- For Sv32, PAGESIZE=2^12 and LEVELS=2.
- The satp register must be active, i.e., the effective privilege mode must be S-mode or U-mode.
- Implementations may also execute the address-translation algorithm speculatively at any time, for any virtual address, as long as satp is active (as defined in Section 12.1.11). Such speculative executions have the effect of pre-populating the address-translation cache.
- Since the algorithm may only touch page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in satp, the range of addresses that an implementation's page-table walker will touch is fully under supervisor control.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: In the virtual address translation process, a is defined as satp.ppn multiplied by PAGESIZE.
RULE 2: The satp register must be active, meaning that the effective privilege mode must be S-mode or U-mode.
RULE 3: Speculative execution of the address-translation algorithm at any time for any virtual address is permitted as long as satp is active.
RULE 4: The address-translation algorithm may only touch page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in satp.
RULE 5: The range of addresses that an implementation's page-table walker will touch is fully under supervisor control because the address-translation algorithm may only touch page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in satp.

The following description:

- The counter-enable (`scounteren`) CSR is a 32-bit register that controls the availability of the hardware performance monitoring counters to U-mode.
- `scounteren` must be implemented.
- When the CY, TM, IR, or HPM n bit in the `scounteren` register is clear, attempts to read the `cycle`, `time`, `instret`, or `hpmcountern` register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
- When one of these bits is set, access to the corresponding register is permitted.
- Any of the bits may be read-only zero, indicating reads to the corresponding counter will cause an exception when executing in U-mode.
- Hence, they are effectively WARL fields.
- The setting of a bit in `mcounteren` does not affect whether the corresponding bit in `scounteren` is writable.
- However, U-mode may only access a counter if the corresponding bits in `scounteren` and `mcounteren` are both set.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The scounteren CSR is a 32-bit register.
RULE 2: The scounteren CSR controls the availability of the hardware performance monitoring counters to U-mode.
RULE 3: scounteren must be implemented.
RULE 4: When the CY bit in scounteren is clear, an attempt to read the cycle register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 5: When the TM bit in scounteren is clear, an attempt to read the time register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 6: When the IR bit in scounteren is clear, an attempt to read the instret register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 7: When the HPM n bit in scounteren is clear, an attempt to read the hpmcountern register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 8: When the CY bit in scounteren is set, access to the cycle register is permitted.
RULE 9: When the TM bit in scounteren is set, access to the time register is permitted.
RULE 10: When the IR bit in scounteren is set, access to the instret register is permitted.
RULE 11: When the HPM n bit in scounteren is set, access to the hpmcountern register is permitted.
RULE 12: Any bit in scounteren may be read-only zero.
RULE 13: A bit in scounteren being read-only zero indicates that reads to the corresponding counter will cause an exception when executing in U-mode.
RULE 14: The bits of scounteren are effectively WARL fields.
RULE 15: The setting of a bit in mcounteren does not affect whether the corresponding bit in scounteren is writable.
RULE 16: U-mode may access a counter only if the corresponding bits in scounteren and mcounteren are both set.

The following description:

- The text states: "When V=1, a virtual-instruction exception (code 22) is normally raised instead of an illegal-instruction exception if the attempted instruction is HS-qualified but is prevented from executing when V=1 either due to insufficient privilege or because the instruction is expressly disabled by a supervisor or hypervisor CSR such as scounteren or hcounteren."

- In that same list: in VU-mode, if XLEN=32, attempts to access a high-half counter CSR when the corresponding bit in either hcounteren or scounteren is 0 and the same bit in mcounteren is 1; the stated effect is that a virtual-instruction exception is raised.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: When V=1, a virtual-instruction exception (code 22) is normally raised instead of an illegal-instruction exception if an attempted HS-qualified instruction is prevented from executing because the instruction is expressly disabled by a supervisor or hypervisor CSR such as scounteren or hcounteren.
RULE 2: In VU-mode, an attempt to access a non-high-half counter CSR raises a virtual-instruction exception when the corresponding bit in either hcounteren or scounteren is 0 and the same bit in mcounteren is 1.
RULE 3: In VU-mode, when XLEN=32, an attempt to access a high-half counter CSR raises a virtual-instruction exception when the corresponding bit in either hcounteren or scounteren is 0 and the same bit in mcounteren is 1.
